  - Autopilot charges (`#charges`, `runChargesFile`) (c): `chargeRun`, `runEvents`, `endRun` and `autopilotNotice` are called synchronously by the coordinator (`ipc.ts:1206-1275`).
  - omp restart, extension apply and the sign-in callback (d): their decisions are the pool's (`omp-restart.ts`, `extension-reload.ts`) and `local/settings.ts`'s (`loopbackCallback`).
  - The `host.*` dispatch (`call`, about 60 methods): a zod parse and one delegation each, to code that already moved (session index 3c, memory 7e, terminals 6b) or stays main's (`machine-services.ts`, `workspace-fs.ts`, `agent-control/branches.ts`, the relays R8 kept). Only uploads (`#parts`: `UPLOAD_PART_IDLE_MS`, `UPLOAD_PARTS_PER_DEVICE`, about 60 lines) has state of its own; it passes §1.3 alone, but in the core each part's bytes would cross the pipe twice (in from the link through main, out again), so it is no unit.
  - Event forwarding (`#wire`, `#emit`, `#sender`, `sendTabEvent`, `#encodedRead`), R6a (c): its input is the sidecar mirror's events after main's own rewrites (`autopilot_update` overlaid by the coordinator, `sidecar-factory.ts:301`; `queue_update` projected onto the spawn relay's ids, `sidecar.ts:1073`; `#statusArg`'s `omp` id and `processAlive`; `#newCommands`' repeat check), and main still parses every lane frame for the pool, run tracking, asks, host calls, quota tokens, model waits and summaries.
  - Measured (13 Nov 2026, this MacBook, shared with two other projects' jobs; raw output kept in `/tmp/r6-probe/`, not in the repository): a throwaway probe on this branch's base ran the rig of `link-traffic-test-rig.ts` (the core's lanes from H1, a real `LocalHostServices`, a real `RemoteSidecar`, both ends in one process) under a CPU profile at 100 µs, self time attributed to the innermost frame of `src/main`, `src/shared` or `resources/gui-bridge`, mean of 7 turns:
    | reply | main busy | lane frames in (`FrameReader.push`, `#onFrame`'s `JSON.parse`, `#receive`, `#dispatchFrame`, bookkeeping strip, batcher) | host link encoder (`link-events.ts`, `message-update-slim.ts`) | `JSON.stringify` and frame (`wire.ts` `send`) and pipe write (`CoreProcess.push`) | device side | GC |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | 450 tokens, 60 link frames | 59.6 ms | ~12.5 ms | ~0.4 ms | ~0.9 ms | ~2.5 ms | 3.4 ms |
    | 900 tokens, 118 | 91.6 ms | ~27.7 ms | ~0.5 ms | ~1.0 ms | ~6 ms | 8.8 ms |
    | 1 800 tokens, 246 | 179.6 ms | ~59 ms (`FrameReader.push` 20.0, `#onFrame` 29.4) | ~0.9 ms | ~1.3 ms | ~13 ms | 22.2 ms |
    The rest is the harness and profiler (~50 ms at 1 800 tokens) and unattributed `(program)`. The host's link work is 1.2 % of main's busy time. `OMP_GUI_FANOUT_CPU=<dir> OMP_GUI_FANOUT_WINDOWS=1 OMP_GUI_FANOUT_PAGES=0 OMP_GUI_FANOUT_COMPUTERS=0|1|2 OMP_GUI_FANOUT_PROFILE=1 bunx vitest run src/main/remote/fanout-cpu-report.test.ts` (5 956 pre-parsed events: a 1 200-token reply, 4 subagents for 12 s, a 300-line tool; median of 7 runs' thread CPU): 13.7, 32.0, 44.9 ms for 0, 1, 2 computers; per computer the profile puts 12.3 ms in link encode (two: 20.4), 2.0 in JSON and frame (4.3), 0.5 in the pipe; the harness's `SinkLink` stringifies each message again for its byte count, so the CPU step is high by about the JSON line.
  - What R6a would save, and the order it would have to keep: main's encoder, stringify and pipe write, about 2 ms per 1 800-token turn per computer and about 15 ms per computer over the fan-out stream, while the core pays about five times as much per event (R4). Today every tab event of a connection is one sequence (main's `Link.send` → `remote.send` pushes, handled in order), and the decoders mirror it: (1) the frame-derived tab events of the same lane, each after the sender flushes (`extensionUi`, `hostToolCall`, `hostUriRequest`, `promptResult` and `sessionSettled` after the `agent_end` they settle, `commandsUpdate`, `liveFrame`, `btwFrame`, `configUpdate`, `modelCatalogUpdate`, `commandOutput`, `sessionInfoUpdate`, `extensionError`); (2) lane state main relays (`status` with its `omp` id, `session`, `bridge` after the capabilities probe); (3) main's own (`run`, `openAsks`, `signIn`, `awaitingConnection`, `scheduledRetry`, the pending host calls on attach, `host-services.ts:1670-1692`; `viewEnded`, `sessionDeleted`, `agentReview`, the Autopilot notice); (4) a `tab.command`'s `res` after the frames before the lane's answer (`beforeResponse`, `:2015`) and before those after it, since a `get_state` or transcript page is a snapshot an earlier-arriving later event is lost under; (5) the frames main rewrites first (above). A core encoder sees each frame before main does, so anything main makes can land behind lane output it must precede (a `tab.open`'s `openAsks` after a cancel the device already applied leaves a stuck ask). The one sound design found is a watermark: lane frames numbered per tab on both sides, main pushing `mark(n)` (and its rewritten frames) where it calls `sender.events`/`subagentFrame` today, the core feeding its `Sender` only up to the mark. It would be a new `sidecar.frame` protocol in H1/H2's files for 1.2 % of main's time, and a throwaway state: once the pool consumes lane events in the core, the sender sits beside it on one thread, every tab event is made there in order, and the marks are deleted.
  - Measured beside it, not this unit's: main's largest streaming cost, for a host's tab and a window's alike, is reading the lane's raw omp frames, ~59 ms plus most of 22 ms GC of 179.6 ms at 1 800 tokens (12.5 of 59.6 at 450, 27.7 of 91.6 at 900), growing with the square of the reply since each raw `message_update` carries the whole partial message twice (R7: ~12.4 MB of whole events at 1 800 tokens). If the lane sent main R4's slim stream (`slim::Slimmer` and `without_frame_bookkeeping` in process) and main rebuilt whole events with `createLinkEventDecoder` (R7: 0.94 ms parse and decode for 323 KB at 1 800 tokens), main would save roughly 50 ms a 1 800-token turn (inferred, not measured end to end). Done and measured: "Slim lane stream" below.
  - The next attempt: when the pool and turn resume are hub instances (9d-9f), `LocalHostServices` becomes one more hub kind beside `pool`, its synchronous readers become hub calls, the core's lane feeds one `encoder::Sender` per tab and connection (R6a with no watermark), and `host-services.test.ts` runs against it. Until then turn resume reaches it through main.
- **Ports. A generic port to main** (`crates/core/src/ports.rs`, main's client `src/main/core/ports.ts` `CorePort`; batch H3, the pattern of 3a's snapshot generalised): a subsystem in the core that still needs what main owns (its state, read synchronously in the TypeScript, and its code: Electron, the pool, the windows) holds one `Port<S>` per live object main opened. The core asks with the event `port` `{port, ask, method, args}` and main answers with the request `ports.answer` `{port, ask, ok, value | message, code?}`; a tell is the same event with no `ask` and reaches main in the order the core sent it. Main's state is a snapshot it takes at the moment it observes a change and pushes (`ports.push {port, seq, state}`), and also hands with every answer (`state`, `seq`), so a step that resumes after an ask reads main as it was when main answered, never older; the numbers keep an older snapshot from replacing a newer one. After a wait on the core's own clock a step asks `fresh()`, as the TypeScript read the state after its timer. `ports.held {port}` answers once the port closed (`ports.close`, or the subsystem dropped it); a core that dies fails it, which is how main learns its port is gone. An ask pending when the port closes fails with `port_closed`; main's thrown `code` travels to the core's `PortError`.
  - Order: `ports.answer`, `ports.push` and `ports.close` act in their handler's synchronous part, which the server runs in the read loop (9a's `begin`), so a step reads main's answers and snapshots in the order main wrote them; no call waits a push's round trip first. `changes()` hands a waiter every snapshot main sent, in order, not only the latest: a state that held between two pushes is seen by the waiter that was waiting for it (Rust test `a_waiter_sees_a_state_that_held_between_two_pushes`; the TypeScript's `await observe` saw it at the moment it held). `ports.rs` has 3 Rust tests of its own and test hooks that play main; the subsystems' tests (`agentctl::service`, `agentctl::runtime`, `agentctl::app`) take port ids of their own.
  - What stays TypeScript, and why: every state read and every effect on Electron, the pool, the windows, the preferences and the remote link, because they are main's; main builds the snapshot and answers the asks. A subsystem opens its port with its own `*.open` method (`agentControl.open`, `app.open`, `autopilot.open`), which carries the first snapshot and `seq`.
  - Differences: what a TypeScript caller read at once, the core reads from main's last snapshot (a hop behind a change main has not pushed yet; the asks that depend on the arguments, `getPref`, `device`, `callerTitle`, `text`, are asks, not snapshot reads); a core that restarts loses its ports, and each client opens its subsystem again on the next call.
- **9g. Agent-control journals and mail rules** (`crates/core/src/agentctl/{journal,rules}/`; batch H3; was `call-journal.ts`, `send-outbox.ts`, `mail-outbox.ts`, `session-fates.ts`, `pending-cleanups.ts`, `custody.ts`, `pickup.ts`, `auto-close.ts`, `tab-close.ts` (all deleted) and the Mailbox and texts of `mail.ts`, which keeps `MAIL_KINDS`, `deliveryFor`, `MailDelivery`): the five files the service keeps across launches (`journal/`: the call journal, the send outbox, the mail outbox, the session fates, the pending cleanups; JSON arrays in `<userData>`, written as the TypeScript wrote them: `disk/tree.rs` `write_atomic_if`, mode 0600 where the TypeScript pinned one, keys in the order of its object literals through `preserve_order`, numbers spelled as `JSON.stringify` spells them) and the rules the service reads synchronously (`rules/`: `mail.rs` the `Mailbox` and its texts, `custody.rs`, `pickup.rs` with `parse_worker_result`, `auto_close.rs`, `tab_close.rs`). Built first, not called; 9h–9k's cutover calls them.
  - One close rule: `rules/tab_close.rs` (`tab_close_step`, `needs_person_turn`) decides every agent close; main's `tab-close.ts` is deleted, and `AgentControlHost.release` (`host.ts`) asks again, after the release's awaits, only whether the page now holds unsaved Files edits (`edits(...) !== "dirty"`). `web-close-reports`' `@code` tags and `covers` point at `tab_close_step`, the Rust `close_tab` (`service/sessions/stop.rs`) and that re-check.
  - What stays TypeScript, and why: nothing of these files; their one seam is the clock (`Clock`) and two traits, `send_outbox::OwedDeps` (the receiver's omp) and `mail_outbox::RedeliverDeps` (the tab pool), which the service implements over its port.
  - Kept exactly, with tests: tables captured from the TypeScript before deletion and replayed (`journal/captured.json`, `rules/captured.json`): the five journals' scripts (call journal 9, send outbox 5, send delivery 7, mail outbox 5, mail redelivery 2, session fates 7, pending cleanups 5), mail texts 42, mailbox scripts 11, custody 132, worker results 51, pickup 379, auto-close 2 596 and tab-close 108 rows; 23 Rust tests in `journal/tests.rs` and 21 in `rules/tests.rs` run them, and carry the cases of the deleted `call-journal`, `send-outbox`, `mail-outbox`, `custody`, `pickup`, `auto-close`, `tab-close` and `mail` suites.
  - Differences: `session-fates` and `pending-cleanups` wrote with `writeFileAtomicDeferred` (one attempt at once, a refused rename retried on a timer, a newer write calling the older off); here each write is one blocking `write_atomic_if` (the retries sleep in the caller, on Windows only), so there is nothing to call off and writes land in order. A file with a lone UTF-16 surrogate escape (`"\ud800"`) is unreadable to `serde_json` where `JSON.parse` takes it: it reads as damaged (nothing recorded); no writer here produces one. A number `JSON.stringify` prints with an exponent (`1e+21`) prints as `1e21`. `CallJournal.begin` takes no `acked` (the service never passed one) and `get` returns a copy. `Mailbox`'s unused `now` dependency is gone and its timers are tokio's (a generation number retires a replaced timer; a flush already delivering is never cancelled, as before). `rules::tab_close::FileEdits` has no serde, so the seam's `types::FileEdits` converts. `BranchFacts` and `FileChange` are git's own types (`git/branches.rs`, counts `u64`; `uncommitted`, `untracked`, `base_uncommitted` `usize`): one definition, with `as f64` or integer comparisons at the use sites.
- **9h–9j. The agent-control service** (`crates/core/src/agentctl/service/`; batch H3; was `src/main/agent-control/service.ts`, class `AgentControl`, 5 173 lines to a typed client): the skeleton, then the three tool families, cut as the doc's §2.4 says, by family. `service/mod.rs` (`AgentControl`: `Arc<Inner>` with `Mem`, the one lock over every map the class kept, never held across an await; the call pipeline `run`/`outcome`/`reconcile`/`dispatch`; the wire), `host.rs` (the seam: `HostState`, the asks and tells over one `Port`), `tuning.rs` (every wait, in ms, defaults the TypeScript's), `resolve.rs` (`find`, `fate_of`, `stored_session`, `resolve_any`, `open`, devices and links, `when_ready`), `state.rs` (a session's state line, pickup, names), `schedules.rs` (`ScheduleSource`), the pure functions (`text.rs`, `wrap.rs`, `js.rs`, `util.rs`), `fail.rs`/`types.rs`; **sessions** (`sessions.rs`: `list`, stored list, `result`, `read`; `sessions/open.rs`: `open_commands`, `open_many`, `open_one`, `open_text`, `ensure_started`, `open_sessions`; `sessions/stop.rs`: `stop`, `stop_stored`, `person_turn`, `abort`, `delete_when_idle`, `journal_close`, `drop_worktree`, `when_gone`, `close_tab`, `fated`, `remember_closed`; `pace.rs`: `Pace`, `waste`, `pace_short`, `pace_line` of `session-pace.ts`); **messages** (`messages.rs`: `send`, `until_draft_sent`, `deliver_send`, `hand_send`, `redeliver_sends`, `journal_marks`, `turn_starts`, `report_fields`, `report`, `queued_note`, `answer`, `answer_plan`, `child_asked`, `person_typed`, with `Owed` implementing `OwedDeps`; the page's `approvalChoices` rule is copied as `approval_once`); **work** (`work.rs`, `work/{review,auto,schedule,app}.rs`: `compare`, `merge`, `schedule`, `autopilot`, `app`, `review_items`, `review`, `family_edits`, `live_review`, `auto_close`, `auto_done`, the work card's cost split and transcript, `delete_throwaway`).
  - Wire: `agentControl.open {port, seq?, state, files: {fates?, cleanups?, calls?: {file}, sendOutbox?: {file}}, caps: {reviewChanged, sessionPace, sentMarks, beforeFirstPrompt, autopilot, appPorts}, tuning, runtime?}`, `agentControl.call {port, method, args}` (the TypeScript public method names: `run`, `childrenOf`, `childAsked`, `clearQueue`, `released`, `openSessions`, `openTarget`, `personTyped`, `redeliverSends`, `reviewItems`, `review`, `familyEdits`, `liveReview`, `autoClose`), `agentControl.close {port}`. A failure's code is `tool_error` (a `ToolError` or a deleted session), `folder_missing`, else main's code or `error`. Main answers `args.parse` (zod's output, `loose` or not), `mail`, `command`, `spawn`, `release`, `exited`, `markAnswered`, `deleteSession`, `deleteStored`, `confirm`, `answer`, `stored`, `storedMessages`, `sessionPace`, `branchFacts`, `landing`, `branchEvidence`, `mergeBranch`, `removeSafeWorktree`, `discardWorktree`, `sentMarks`, `beforeFirstPrompt`, `autopilot.{plan,start,status,stop}`, `schedules.{command,create}`, `appPorts.*`, and takes the tells `start`, `setTask`, `view`, `notify`, `reviewChanged`, `recordClosed`, `appPorts.fire|send`. The snapshot `HostState` (camelCase JSON, every field defaulted) holds the switches, the devices and their states, the open tabs once each in pool order (without `here`), `windowOf`, outside callers, `parents`, `checkoutPeers`, `summaries` per computer, `stopping`, tombstones, the computers with a Schedules service, the language's texts as templates (`fillText` fills them, plural slots included) and `app` (9l's `AppState`). `AgentControl.run`, `openSessions` and the other public methods are asynchronous in the client; `childrenOf`, `released` and `personTyped`, which were synchronous, are too.
  - What stays TypeScript, and why: `tools.ts` (the zod and JSON schemas omp receives; `skill-drift.test.ts` pins them against the skill) and the `args.parse` handler that runs them; `branches.ts`' `mergeProblem`/`boundDiff` stay beside `remote/host-services.ts`' `mergeBranch` and `branchEvidence`, which use them (the core has its own copies for the tools); `host.ts` keeps everything that reads the pool, windows, preferences, the remote link or Electron (9k lists it); `service.ts` is the seam and typed client (`AgentControlHost`, `AgentControl` over a `CorePort`, `AppPorts`, which moved into it).
  - Kept exactly, with tests: `service.test.ts`, `host.test.ts`, the rest of `agent-control/*.test.ts` and `control-socket/*.test.ts` run unchanged in their assertions against the real core (578 pass, twice in a row after a rebuild); tables captured from the TypeScript before the pure functions were deleted (`service/captured.json`: 59 functions, about 800 cases, among them `oneLine`, `head`, `cut`, `wrapAgentMessage`, `parseAgentMessage`, the dispatch and request marks, `renderMessage`, `fillText`, `describeRun`, `startOptions`, `mutates`, `callIntent`, `parseVerdict`; `service/work/captured.json`: turn edits 6, cost split 4, `timeoutForCommand` 59); 55 Rust tests under `agentctl::service` (sessions 9, pace 3, messages 4 and the families' own). Cases of deleted pure functions (`elapsedText`, `currentStep`, `renderMessage` where it was asserted, `checksRun`, `parseVerdict`, `paceShort`) are the Rust tables' now; `paceLine`, `waste`, `toolCalls` and `toolErrors` stay in `session-pace.ts` for `scripts/session-pace.ts`. The tests' fake timers became `tuning`'s short waits and real sleeps (the matrix's device return 2 s → 100 ms, the abort delay 12 s → 200 ms, in the same ratio), `FakeHost` gives `here` as a window key and the schedules as a `command`/`create` service, and five call-journal cases touch the journal file instead of a `CallJournal`.
  - Differences: a cut that lands inside a surrogate pair (`outputTail`'s tail, `agentTaskTitle`'s 119th character) leaves a lone half in JavaScript and U+FFFD here (`head`, `cut` and `oneLine` never split a pair, as before); `journalMarks` in `dispatch` is `u64` and saturates where JavaScript used a float; `ControlTab.here` is computed in the core, not by main; `parentOf` is a lookup in `HostState.parents`, not a `ControlTab` field; the schedules' `rowOf`, `list`, `save`, `update`, `setEnabled` and `runs` logic is the core's `ScheduleSource` and main keeps `schedules.command`/`create`, so a call makes at least one request to the Schedules service (a create includes a get); `nameUntitled`'s lookup is a spawned task: when the wait for it stops (`name_lookup_ms`) `setTask` still goes; `within` drops its future when the time passes, where the TypeScript went on with the promise (what it already sent to main still goes, nothing more is waited for); a failed open's tab close (no worktree) is a spawned task so the caller does not wait (it has no recorded-call scope); the stored list sorts by bytes, not `localeCompare` (ISO times, same order); `pace_short`'s `toFixed(1)` rounds exact ties up as JavaScript does; an `open_many` reservation key (`pending:…`) is a counter and the time, not `Math.random`; `result` waits `poll_ms` (3 000), as the TypeScript's `POLL_MS` did, not the skeleton comment's `result_poll_ms`; `person_typed`'s note is a spawned task (the TypeScript's `void … catch`); a call that joins a request id whose first call vanished before it finished fails with "The first call of this request did not finish; send it again." (the TypeScript awaited the first call's promise); a failing `sent` read in an owed-send check reads as not asked and a failing `drained` as false (the TypeScript's rejection reached `send`); `Promise.all` is `util::join::all` on the calling task, never `tokio::spawn`, so the recorded call scope (`CALL_KEY`) is kept. Main stamps a turn (`busySince`) and the core stamps a report with two processes' clocks, which on Windows may read a tick (about 16 ms) apart: "the same turn" is therefore told by main's own stamp, not by comparing times (`Mem::reported_in`, `mailed_in`: the child's `busySince` as main saw it at the report or mail; a turn with another stamp is one it carried on with), where the TypeScript compared the report's time with `busySince` in one clock; pinned by "stops listing a child as waiting once it carries on with a new turn" (a new turn main stamps 10 ms before the core's report time still counts). A schedule's `in_minutes` and the other times the core writes read the core's clock, so a test that bounds one with its own `Date.now()` allows that tick (`ompdesk_schedule` "creates once …"). The test fake's tombstones are written as `deleted-sessions.ts` writes them, resolved by node's `path.resolve`, and the core looks a local one up the same way (`host.rs` `node_resolve`).
- **9k. The agent-control host's sweeps** (`crates/core/src/agentctl/runtime/`; batch H3; was the sweeps, review pushes, redelivery and wake of `src/main/agent-control/host.ts`, class `AgentControlRuntime`): the opened sessions' sweep and its timers (`SWEEP_*`: one `autoClose` pass per opener 1 s after tab activity, every 10 s for every opener; `#sweepSoon`, `#sweepAll`, `#openers`), the review board's settle and push (`REVIEW_SETTLE_MS` 400 ms; `#pushReview`, `#sendReview`'s cache and `listedIn`, `#moved`, `#sweepReviews`, `liveReview`), pool activity's effects (a child's question card tells its opener, a released tab goes through `AgentControl::released`), the redelivery of reports and messages whose receiver's journal does not hold them (`#redeliverMail`, `#redeliverSends`; the mail outbox's keep and settle are `Host::mail`'s), a routine's wake-up (`wakeSession`: the wait for the turn to start and the end), and `forgetDeleted`'s verdict and `acknowledge` (the call journal). Opened with the service (`agentControl.open` with a `runtime` section; wire `agentRuntime.*` on the same port); main hands the core every tab activity (`agentRuntime.activity`) after pushing its snapshot. Tells to main: `reviewSent`, `nextPromptChildren`, `closedChildren`, `wakeDone`; ask: `reach`.
  - The two rules `branches.ts` kept for the service's synchronous reads moved with 9k into `git/branches.rs` (`merge_problem`, `bound_diff`, one copy the service and the git requests share; `WorktreeRef` is git's one type): `git.branchEvidence` takes `diffChars` and answers the diff already cut (`{diff, diffOmitted, merge}`, where main cut a raw diff of up to 4 MiB after it crossed the pipe), and `git.mergeCheck` (facts, the refusal as a `branch_error`, the prediction, in one request) is the start of `mergeBranch`, whose TypeScript runs only the hooks around the core's merge steps now. The `boundDiff` cases of `branches-worktrees.test.ts` are Rust tests beside it; `branches-merge.test.ts` and `host-services.test.ts` pass unchanged through the core.
  - What stays TypeScript, and why: the snapshot builder (`#hostTabs`, `#controlTab`, `#openerOf`, `#autoClosable`), the host handlers (`#spawn`, `#worktree*`, `#git`, `#confirm`, `#appPorts`, `#hostCommand`, `#sendMail`), `#reach` and `#recordClosed` (a pref write), the outside callers' books (`#outside`, `#outsideMail`, `#outsideCards`), `#windows`, `#parents`, `#userPrompted`, `#autopilotRuns`: each reads the pool, the windows, the preferences, the remote link or Electron, or is main's state the snapshot is built from. Main pushes the snapshot at every pool activity (step included), `prefChanged`, a summaries change, a remote host coming online, a session deleted, an outside caller entering or leaving, `noteUserPrompt`, and after a tell with a side effect (`start`, `setTask`, `view`, `recordClosed`, `fire`, `closedChildren`).
  - Differences: the mail's receiver tab is found in the snapshot by session path, not by the pool's `sessionOwner`; each sweep tick asks `fresh()` once; a service with a runtime opens in its constructor, and a core that ended opens it again a second later; the Autopilot stopping flag and a device going offline have no push hook: each call's snapshot and the core's `fresh()` read stand for them (a remote's offline transition is found by `fresh()` and polling); every pool activity pushes the whole snapshot (not measured under load); when a tab's status shows its omp ended while a command of an open is outstanding, the core asks main once for its state and takes main's answer if main had already given it (the status can reach the core before the rejection of the command it ended). A failed open's close starts before the open answers, as the TypeScript's call ran to its first await; ports apply main's answers and snapshots in line order (Ports above), so there is no pre-call push round trip and no grace for an open's rejection.
  - Kept exactly, with tests: `host.test.ts` runs against the real core; its timer cases poll in real time, and a restart case closes the earlier `runtime.control`; 12 Rust tests in `agentctl::runtime` (two `host.test.ts` cases, "no new timer review while `review` reads" and "only the moved child is read again", moved there because the core calls its own `review`); `bridge-agent-mail-restart-stock.test.ts` keeps an array where it used `MailOutbox`, the outbox pass being the Rust module test's.

A call's timeout is `2^31 - 1` ms (a card may wait).

The `prefTier`/`settingTier`/`rpcTier` direct assertions go through `app.tier`; the two cases that gave `AppControl` a registry of closures use a `ScriptedAction {tier, steps: [getPref|setPref|rpc|invoke]}` handed in `app.open`'s `scripts`; `control.list` is awaited in five places.
  - Differences: `list` is asynchronous; each `list` and `run` starts with one `fresh()` and reads the snapshot (`manage`, `full`, the language, the sidebar) from then on; `AppRefusal` carries `code = "app_refusal"`, a refusal thrown by main's handler arrives with it and the final message is unchanged, any other error is a `CoreError` (code kept, same message); an MCP or provider sign-in starts its first poll at once, then spawns, so its command leaves before the run's answer, as the synchronous `ports.rpc` did (the 3 s early window is unchanged); a number is spelled as JavaScript spells it, except an exponent (1e21 and over, below 1e-6) is not implemented; JSON keys keep their order through `preserve_order`, and integer-like keys are not moved first as JavaScript does; `toFixed(1)` ties round up as in JavaScript; `settings.get` asks its pref rows one after another, not at once (same result).
  - CI coverage: `mac-native` runs `src/main/agent-control/*.test.ts` (the service, host, app-control, app-catalog, branches and skill-drift suites) against the real macOS core (`.github/actions/native-parity`, which lists it); `check` and `win-platform` run them in the node project; `native` runs the Rust tests on all three OSes. Built and run on macOS arm64 (`bun run build:native`); the Windows paths are CI's checks. No performance claim.
  - Formal: `formal/web-close-reports` `@code` tags and `covers` point at `rules/tab_close.rs` (`tab_close_step`), `service/sessions/stop.rs` (`close_tab`) and `host.ts`' release re-check; the run passes (matrix 3 of 3, 8 of 8 mutations detected, tags resolve) and reports DRIFT against its sync commit, which is re-stamped after the batch run, not here.
- **Slim lane stream** (`crates/core/src/sidecar/stream.rs`, `lane.rs` `emit_frame`/`cleanup`, `src/main/sidecar.ts` `#onFrame`; batch W2, decided by the main session on 13 Nov 2026 from R6's "Measured beside it"): the lane sends main a slim stream made by R4's one slimmer, and main rebuilds whole events with the one decoder, `createLinkEventDecoder`, before anything in main reads them. No second copy of either. A `message_update` leaves the lane without its request bookkeeping (`without_frame_bookkeeping`), the first of each message whole and every later one reduced (`slim::Slimmer`); every other frame is omp's line as it wrote it. A `subagent_event` stays whole: the decoder cannot rebuild its snapshot (it restores only the usage), and `LocalHostServices` sends `subagentFrame` as main emits it to a device without `slimEvents` (`host-services.ts` `#emit`), which `link-traffic-report.test.ts` pins (it failed while the lane reduced them). `SidecarManager` runs each `message_update` through its decoder right after the bookkeeping strip in `#onFrame`, so the batcher, the pool (`#wireLight` → the hub's `feed`, the per-window slimmers), `trackRunEvent`, the asks, the quota, the summaries and the link sender see whole events as before. The pool needed no change.
  - Where the decoder could lack a base, the lane starts the slimmer over (`Stream::reset`): at a message's `message_end` and at `agent_end` (where the decoder forgets its own), and in `Lane::cleanup`, which `restart`, `kill`, `sleep` and `dispose` run first. Those are the commands main fences frames out on (`life`), and a held `agent_end` and its tail are released after the reset. A new lane (a new core, a reopened tab) starts with a new slimmer. Main drops a stale frame before decoding it, and a decode miss cannot occur: a reduced update only follows a whole one of the same message on the same lane since the last reset.
  - In-core subscribers (`sidecar::subscribe`, H2) get the slim frame, the very bytes main gets: the in-core consumer to come (R6a, R4's encoder in process) slims the same way.
  - Measured (13 Nov 2026, this MacBook, load average 11-14 from two other projects' jobs; throwaway probe, not kept: the rig of `link-traffic-test-rig.ts`, a host's tab streamed to a device in one process, before and after interleaved in blocks of three turns by swapping the core binary between blocks, 12 turns each, median; main's thread CPU per turn, with the CPU profiler at 100 µs running, and the profile's busy, GC and frames-in time, frames in being `FrameReader.push`, `#receive`, `sidecar.ts`, the bookkeeping strip, the batcher and the decoder; main's TypeScript was the after code in both, so before also pays the decoder's pass over whole updates):
    | reply | thread CPU before → after | main busy | GC | frames in |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | 450 tokens | 179.6 → 173.9 ms | 106.6 → 104.8 | 10.0 → 1.0 | 32.3 → 25.5 |
    | 900 tokens | 361.5 → 330.3 ms | 184.9 → 155.4 | 15.5 → 3.6 | 73.9 → 49.4 |
    | 1 800 tokens | 764.5 → 663.4 ms | 406.1 → 258.5 | 80.0 → 4.6 | 156.1 → 90.5 |
    At 1 800 tokens the rounds do not overlap (before 742-877 ms thread CPU, after 646-730); `#onFrame` fell 73.2 → 29.7 ms and `FrameReader.push` 64.6 → 43.3 (it also reads the link's frames to the device). The absolute numbers are about twice R6's on this machine's load; the deltas are this unit's.
  - Kept exactly, with tests: `stream.rs`'s tests (the first update whole without bookkeeping, the next reduced to what `slim_message_update` keeps, a frame with nothing to slim byte for byte as omp wrote it, `message_end`/`agent_end`/reset sending the next update whole, a subagent's event as omp wrote it, the tab's own slimmer unmoved) and the lane test `main_and_subscribers_get_a_messages_later_updates_reduced_and_a_restarted_omps_first_whole` (a real lane and stand-in omp: the second update reduced, a restart's first whole, the subscriber's bytes equal main's). `link-traffic-report.test.ts` (the bytes a slim and an older device get), `sidecar.test.ts`, `session-launch-parity.test.ts`, `link-events.test.ts`, `link-events-core.test.ts`, `link-events-stock.test.ts` (real omp), `link-traffic-relaunch.test.ts` and `sidecar-pool.test.ts` run through the lane's stream.
  - Fan-out (`fanout-cpu-report.test.ts`'s replay, every push of every receiver written out and compared, run once, not kept): the stream the unchanged build recorded (5 948 events) against the same stream as main reads it with the slim lane (1 277 updates reduced as the lane reduces them, a JSON round trip, then the decoder): all 679 pushes to each of 3 windows, 509 to each of 2 pages and 673 to each of 2 computers are byte-identical. The test's own window and page hashes differ between two runs of the very same stream, because each vitest process runs in its own temp folder (`scripts/run-temp.ts`) and `tab:status` carries the folder as `cwd` and `sessionPath`; with that folder written as one name, every receiver hashes the same in both runs and after (windows `ced72d1782bd21fc`, pages `9ce9462e8a2401f8` and `66f05c360d2c2867`, computers `c1d98f748931f62c`).
  - Differences: a window that attaches mid-message, or that `resnapshotWindow` reset, gets as its whole update the decoder's rebuild (`role`, `timestamp`, `usage`, the text, thinking and tool call blocks; no `api`, `provider`, `model`, `stopReason`, no `partial`), as a paired device's window already did; the renderer reads only `content` and `timestamp` of a streaming message (`stores/messages.ts`, `ChatStream.tsx` `liveProcessParts`), and the committed message (`message_end`) crosses unchanged. A device older than v0.46.0 (no `slimEvents`) gets those rebuilt updates (the later updates of a message with its rebuilt `content`, no `partial`, no `api`/`provider`/`model`/`stopReason`) instead of stock's whole ones; its own pool reduces every update after a message's first, so its windows get what they got before, except one that attaches mid-message.
  - Binary: `ompdesk-core` 11 469 312 → 11 469 312 bytes (darwin-arm64, the build the node project stages; the growth stays inside a page). Crates: none.
  - CI coverage: `native` runs the Rust tests on all three OSes; `mac-native` already runs `sidecar.test.ts`, `session-launch-parity.test.ts`, `link-events-core.test.ts`, `link-traffic-relaunch.test.ts`, `fanout-cpu-report.test.ts` and `sidecar-pool.test.ts` against the macOS core, and now `link-traffic-report.test.ts` (its frames cross the slim lane); `link-events-stock.test.ts` needs an installed omp and runs where one is.
- **H2's base. The hub** (`crates/core/src/hub/`, `src/main/core/hub.ts`; batch H2, the owner's approval of 12 Nov 2026 with three conditions: a short deadline per call, a measured blocking total, no deadlock with the watchdog or the quit). The pool and turn resume are read and changed by main in the same tick (179 call sites, listeners that change and read), so main calls them **synchronously**: a worker thread of main holds a private connection to the core (a Unix socket in a 0700 `mkdtemp` folder, a Windows pipe with a random name that refuses remote clients; a token proves either way; `OMPDESK_HUB`, `OMPDESK_HUB_TOKEN`, new for every core start) and main's thread blocks on `Atomics.wait` for each answer. In the core one thread runs every hub instance in JavaScript's order: a job (a call, or a timer's or an answer's wake) runs to its end, then every task it made ready, then what they told main goes out, then the next job. A call answers after every item it made; what its woken continuations made after the answer reaches main in a microtask after the call returned, as a promise continuation ran. A rule may ask main synchronously in the middle of a call (`Out::ask`: a sidecar's `start()` that emits back into the pool, a hook); the nested calls that causes are answered meanwhile, as a listener ran inside the TypeScript method. A promise of main's (an RPC, a probe) is `ask_later`, never awaited inside a synchronous call. `hub.sync` is a barrier for a path that acts on omp without calling the core (`SidecarPool.beginMove`).
  - Deadline: `HUB_CALL_DEADLINE_MS` 2 s per wait (the hang watchdog flags 5 s). A missed deadline, or a hub connection that ends, abandons the core (killed and forgotten at once, so the next call starts a new one; the lanes reopen by 9c's path) and tells every instance `lost`; the next call of each re-opens it and re-seeds it (`Hub.callOnce`): the pool from the handles main holds (tab ids, windows, devices, kinds, statuses, session files, full wiring, device views; lost: each tab's run, titles, asks, sign-ins, drafts, subagent levels, counters, splits), turn resume from the marker file and what the pool shows (lost: armed retries, held waits, probes, continuations on their way, which end and tell Autopilot `none`), the connectivity monitor from `net.isOnline()`.
  - Pinned by `core/hub.test.ts` against the real core: answers after their items, a synchronous question with a nested call inside it, a later question, woken continuations as microtasks, a stale worker ping (a drain answers at once), the deadline (blocked 2.04 s, then a new core answers), a core killed mid-call from another process (main wakes in about 250 ms); `hub/tests.rs` the job order and the ask's end at an instance's close; `sidecar-pool.test.ts` "a lost core" (tabs and their session files' ownership survive).
  - Measured (13 Nov 2026, this MacBook, packaged-equivalent build, `OMP_GUI_HUB_STATS=1`, a throwaway Playwright run: 20 tabs, a turn streaming in front, the tab switches every 1.5 s waking background tabs, a second window viewing the streaming tab as a paired page does, a short turn in a background tab every 30 s; a paired computer over the link was not measured): per wait p50 49–172 µs, p99 0.25–1.4 ms, max 15–36 ms (the 20 spawns and first wakes); main blocked 0.54–1.81 s per minute (0.9–3 %), the first minute the highest. Before the summary builder read every tab's facts in one call it was 14 900 calls and 2.0–2.3 s a minute (`summaryFacts` 9 500 of them). Most calls now: `windowOfTab` and `tabsForWindow` (about 1 000 each a minute while tabs switch), `summarySources`+`summaryFactsAll` (about 470 each).
  - Differences: a call that would run longer than 2 s loses the core where the TypeScript ran on (none measured over 36 ms); the hub's thread is one more thread in the core.
- **9d, 9e. The sidecar pool and the saved tab layout** (`crates/core/src/pool/`: `state.rs` the table, `windows.rs` acquire, release and each window's view, `listen.rs` each sidecar event the pool reads, `routes.rs` F-OWN and F-UI-ORIGIN, `api.rs` the public methods, `later.rs` the six that were `async`, `persist.rs` `tabLayoutForWindow`/`restoreLayout`, `layout.rs` was `tab-layout.ts`'s `sanitizePersistedTabLayout(s)`, `run.rs` was `sidecar-run-state.ts`, `split.rs` a copy of `src/shared/split-layout.ts`; batch H2; hub kinds `pool`, `layout`). The core is the one writer of the tab table, each window's active/visible/split/remembered view, session file ownership, request owners and bodies, the answered and answering sets, drafts, subagent levels, sign-in state, pending omp restarts and extension plans, and every tab's run. `SidecarPool` keeps its public methods as the client.
  - What stays TypeScript, and why: the handles (the `SidecarLike` objects, `ClientWindow`s by `webContents.id`, `HostedTabView`s, the full-channel forwarders with their message-update slimmers and subagent batcher: they ride `SidecarManager`'s `EventBatcher`, which 9c kept in main, and Electron's `webContents.send`), the collaborators the core asks (the factory, the hooks, `deletedSessions`, `removalHolds`, `canonicalCwd`, `isWebWindow`, `ompRestartDecision`, `extensionApplyDecision`, `mergeApplyPlans`, `failureOf`, `BLOCKING_UI_METHODS`, `isSignInRequest`, `isApprovalSelect`, `describeAnswer`, main's `setImmediate` and stop timer), `ModelWaitTracker`, `MovesInFlight`, the activity observers, the exit promises; `sidecar-run-state.ts` for `remote/host-services.ts` until R6 (the pool's run is `run.rs`; both pinned to `run-captured.json`, 2 040 steps); `tab-layout.ts` and `web/web-tab-layouts.ts` as the layout's client; `tab-spawn.ts` and `tab-release.ts` unchanged (their order is main's; `closeWindowTab`'s re-check and release stay in one tick, `web-close-reports`). A streaming `message_update` never reaches the core: the client hands it the events its rules read, cut to the fields they read.
  - Kept exactly, with tests: `sidecar-pool.test.ts` 166 cases (165 unchanged, one added), `session-launch-parity.test.ts`, `remote/host-services.test.ts`, `tab-layout.test.ts`, `tab-spawn.test.ts`, `tab-release.test.ts`, `web/web-tab-layouts.test.ts` and the suites that build a pool (`agent-control/*`, `control-socket/*`, `web-push*`, `routines/sessions`, `session-summaries`) run through the real core unchanged; `split.rs` against 785 rows captured from `split-layout.ts`; Rust tests drive the pool through the hub with a scripted main (first tab, F-OWN, run end, a window's close, request routing and the first answer, a restart's end of runs and asks, a layout round trip, the seed with its session files).
  - Differences: a collaborator's thrown error comes back as an `Error` with its message (`already_answered`, `session_running` keep their codes); a run reply preview cut inside a surrogate pair ends in U+FFFD; `entryForWindow` is gone (no caller); a completion report can come a microtask later; the layout's folder, file and content checks are the core's own reads (`deleted` stays main's); a tab acquire that throws still leaves its entry, as before.
  - Not measured: speed against the TypeScript (the blocking totals are above).
  - CI coverage: `mac-native` runs the H2 parity files (`.github/actions/native-parity`); `check` and `win-platform` run them in the node project; `native` runs `pool::`, `hub::` and `sidecar::` Rust tests on all three OSes.
- **9f. Turn resume and the connectivity monitor** (`crates/core/src/resume/`: `machine.rs` was `TurnResume`, `connectivity.rs` `ConnectivityMonitor`, `facts.rs` `deriveMarkers`, `launchAction`, `openedRunning`, the restore summary, `ends.rs` with `hold-ends.json` the end table that `turn-resume-ends.ts` now reads too, `text.rs`/`jsre.rs` the failure classifiers and the quota rules; batch H2; hub kinds `resume`, `connectivity`). Both classes keep their constructors, dependencies and methods as clients; every dependency (the pool, the network, the store, the clock, the timers, `openTab`, `resumeGoal`, `goalSettled`, `log`, the probe) is asked, so the tests' scripted fakes still drive them. `sync()` and `flush()` are each one job with the facts main read in that tick, so the model's assumption that they run in one macrotask holds (`formal/turn-resume` A2); identity the `WeakSet`s compared (outcomes, stock retries, markers) crosses as ids the client gives.
  - What stays TypeScript, and why: `TOUCH_COMMANDS`, `movesTab`, `movesTabUnknown`, `MovesInFlight`, `trackMove` (promises and `RpcTimeoutError` instances), `personsPrompt` (`parseAgentMessage`, the page's), `InterruptedTurnStore` (main's atomic write with its Windows retry), `retryDelays`/`limitLook` (environment parsing), the probe's transport (Electron's uncached resolver, `net.isOnline`, `powerMonitor`); `src/shared/quota-reset.ts` for the page and Autopilot, pinned with `text.rs` to `classifiers-captured.json` (137 messages).
  - Kept exactly, with tests: `turn-resume.test.ts`, `turn-resume-limit.test.ts`, `turn-resume-ends.test.ts`, `connectivity.test.ts`, `autopilot-restart-resume.test.ts` run unchanged through the real core (389 cases).
  - Differences: `goalSettled`, `log` and the row setters are items main applies after the core's stretch, where the TypeScript called them inline; a continuation's later steps run as microtasks after the call that started it; a `changed` of the monitor runs `flush` after the job that went online (for a probe's answer or a timer, in a macrotask of its own: `formal/turn-resume` A17); the core keeps every outcome id it was given (about 8 bytes per finished run).
  - Formal: the `@code` tags of `turn-resume`, `session-listing`, `tab-session` and `session-processes` name the Rust functions (`formal/retag-core.ts` finds a tag's lines again by its function after an edit); new rows A16–A18 (`turn-resume`), A11 (`session-listing`), A6 (`tab-session`). A18, the round trip between a continuation's last check and its command, is closed by the barrier above; the models are run and restamped after the batch's gate.
- **S. The shell swap** (batch S, built on `omp/agent/rust-batch-shell` stacked on H1; lands last, after every other batch and after the macOS half of P agrees; `crates/shell`, its own cargo workspace). Design notes, decided 12 Nov 2026 by the batch's session:
  - **What runs the TypeScript main that is left: Node, as a child of the shell.** `OMPdesk(.exe)` is the Rust CEF shell. It starts Node 24 LTS (shipped in `<resources>/node/`) on the same `out/main/index.js` bundle, whose `electron` import is a shim (`src/main/shell-host/electron`) that speaks the host protocol (`crates/shell/PROTOCOL.md`: the core's wire, one JSON object per line with `bin` frames, requests both ways) over a local socket (`OMPDESK_SHELL_SOCKET`, a named pipe on Windows, a unix socket on macOS, with a per-launch token). Main keeps starting `ompdesk-core` as today. Electron's synchronous getters (`getBounds`, `isMaximized`, `getURL`, `screen`, `nativeTheme`, `app.getPath`, `safeStorage`) answer from state the shell pushes (`win.state`, init facts); the few that cannot be mirrored became async at their callers. The page gets `window.omp` from `src/preload/shell.ts`, which the shell injects before any page script, so `createOmpApi`'s transport is the only seam the page sees, as before.
  - Its cost: on disk Node's binary (`node.exe` 24.22.1, win-x64: 93 915 464 bytes, measured here), on top of CEF's +100 MB over Electron (P); in memory Node's own baseline (an empty script: 33.6 MB RSS, measured here) beside main's heap, which P measured at about 75 MB inside Electron's browser process; every Electron call is a socket round trip or a mirror read. It ends when the hub program (H2–H4, W2) and §4's files have moved into the core and the shell: then Node and the shim are deleted.
  - Why not the others: Electron run as Node ships Electron again (about 200 MB) beside CEF; Bun is not on a machine before setup installs it, and its Node compatibility is not Electron's Node that main was written and tested on; porting the rest first is the hub program itself.
  - Why the shell is the parent: the OS starts `OMPdesk.exe` (shortcuts, the updater's relaunch, `omp://` deep links), so single instance (one pipe per userData) and deep links are the shell's before Node runs; Node's pre-ready code (Chromium switches, privileged schemes, the GPU fallback) runs first and hands them to the shell in `app.init`, and only then does CEF initialize, the order Electron had.
  - **Data**: userData is Electron's folder read as it is (`--user-data-dir`, else `%APPDATA%\ompdesk` / `~/Library/Application Support/ompdesk`); the shell's own Chromium profile is `<userData>/shell-webview/`. The page moves from `file://` to `app://ompdesk`, so the shell imports Electron's `file://` Local Storage (drafts, outbox, snapshot cache, unread marks) once. `safeStorage`: the shell reads the key once (Windows: `os_crypt.encrypted_key` of Electron's `Local State`, DPAPI; macOS: the "ompdesk Safe Storage" keychain item) and hands it to the shim, which encrypts and decrypts byte-identically to Electron's OSCrypt `v10`; a key it cannot read makes `isEncryptionAvailable()` false with the reason logged, and each stored secret is asked for again with that reason shown.
  - **The update move** keeps the release's two asset names and its metadata comment. Windows: no handoff release (P measured it); the new `ompdesk-windows-setup.exe` is the same one-click per-user NSIS identity (uninstall GUID `e6f1f351-…` from appId `com.seuput.ompdesk`, `InstallLocation`, `OMPdesk.exe`), installed by v0.127.0's own NSIS flow. macOS: the relaunch swap's checks (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`, bundle id, version) hold for a CEF bundle signed with the same certificate; open until the macOS half of P has run.
  - **Test seams**: `OMP_GUI_TEST_USER_DATA_DIR` (env, so an updater's argument-less relaunch in a dry run stays on its temp profile), `OMP_GUI_TEST_NODE_INSPECT=1` (Node under `--inspect=127.0.0.1:0`; e2e's launcher runs `evaluate` in main through it, as Playwright's Electron launcher did, and reaches pages over CEF's `--remote-debugging-port`; it never sends CDP `Browser.close`, which quits CEF without main's teardown).
  - **The setup's rollback** (`resources/installer.nsh`): an update keeps the old folder as `<install>.move-backup` until the new folder holds the shell, CEF, Node and the main; an incomplete one is deleted, the old folder comes back with its uninstall entry's values, the setup exits 2 and an automatic update starts the old app again. Its `MessageBox` ("OMPdesk could not be updated: the new files were not complete…") shows only for a setup a person started by hand (not `/S`, not `--updated`); v0.127.0's updater starts the setup without `/S`, so an update's rollback logs to `%TEMP%\ompdesk-setup.log` and the next launch's `swap result … rolled back` line instead. **A real person seeing that box is a failed move.** The only known cause so far is the dry run's forced `fail=swap` (`scripts/shell-move/move-test.nsh` deletes `libcef.dll`), which on 13 Nov 2026 showed two such boxes on the owner's desktop before the box was kept off updates; the move tests now run every setup silent, read the result from the exit code and the log, and fail on any window their processes show.
  - **The keychain on macOS** (13 Nov 2026, the main session under the owner's delegation; it replaces P's open gate). No keychain or credential dialog is ever part of the move, and none falls back to "ask once":
    1. **The real users' path is the gate S lands on**: an "ompdesk Safe Storage" item created by a release build (the self-signed certificate, its stable designated requirement) is read by the new shell, signed with the same certificate, with no SecurityAgent or coreauthd line, on a GitHub macOS runner (never a person's Mac): a release-signed Electron build creates the item and encrypts a stand-in token, the move runs, the shell decrypts it.
    2. **An item made by ad-hoc builds (development machines) with ciphertext present**: the shell reads the key only with user interaction disabled (`--keychain-key` sets `SecKeychainSetUserInteractionAllowed(false)`, so a read that would ask fails `errSecInteractionNotAllowed` and answers `{unavailable, reason: "keychain-would-ask"}`); then nothing touches the keychain again that run: the stored update token is dropped (`GH_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_TOKEN`/`gh auth token` still work), each mail account's password is marked as needing re-entry, and the app shows an in-app notice naming those accounts. No OS dialog, and nothing is lost without the person being told which secret to enter again. Without ciphertext (the owner's MacBook on 13 Nov 2026: no `update-token.bin`, no `mail-accounts/`) the lazy read never runs, so its move shows no dialog at all.
    3. **One item, one rule**: no second service name; the shell reads and writes Electron's item ("ompdesk Safe Storage", account "ompdesk"). A development machine whose item would ask cannot store a new secret either: saving one fails with the same reason shown.
  - **The core is started detached on Windows** (`src/main/core/process.ts`, dc4c6ba36): Node puts every child it starts without `detached` into a job object killed with Node, so a main that crashed or was killed took the core and every omp under it at once, before omp wrote `session_exit` (`e2e/interrupted-resume.e2e.ts:350`; under Electron the same kill left them to end on stdin's end). Detached, the core sees main's stdin end and stops the omps as on every other exit. A detached process has no console, so the core allocates a hidden one first (`sys::ensure_console`, `main.rs`), which its console-program children inherit instead of opening a visible window. `formal/repo-lock-core` covers `process.ts`, `sys.rs` and `main.rs`: it drifts there and is run and restamped with the batch.
  - **The update move, Windows, measured** (13 Nov 2026, this PC, `scripts/shell-move/run-move.ts`, a test identity: GUID `1b7c0f31-…` from the test app id the way electron-builder derives the real one, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\OMPdesk-movetest`, a temp profile, HOME, APPDATA and LOCALAPPDATA; the owner's install, uninstall entry, `omp` handler, shortcuts, `%APPDATA%\ompdesk` top level, updater cache and running processes compared unchanged after each run; every setup silent, no window). The real setup is electron-builder's one-click NSIS over the staged shell as a custom `electronDist` (`dist/ompdesk-<v>-setup.exe`, 175.9 MB; `-DAPP_GUID=e6f1f351-4346-5a64-a104-9a02672ef1bc` read from makensis' arguments; `OMPdesk.exe`, `libcef.dll`, locales `en-US`/`ko` only, `resources/app/out/**` plain, `resources/node/node.exe`). The released v0.127.0's own updater, unchanged, against a stand-in GitHub API: `check found` 0.127.0 → 0.200.0, `download verified` (204 014 897 bytes, SHA-512), `scheduler safe`, `countdown`, `install auto`, `swap helper nsis`; the new shell: `migrate.localStorage` (8 keys, 201 047 bytes: a draft, Korean text, a 200 000-character value), `migrate.token` (Electron's DPAPI-wrapped OSCrypt key), Node from `resources/node/node.exe`, the page from `app://ompdesk/index.html` with `window.omp`; `swap result installed`, the entry `DisplayVersion 0.200.0` with its GUID and `InstallLocation`, no backup or second copy left; its uninstaller removed the folder and both keys and kept userData. With the unpack made incomplete on purpose (`--fail-at swap`) the setup put the old folder back, logged to `%TEMP%\ompdesk-setup.log` with no dialog, and the restarted Electron logged `swap result outcome rolled back, previous 0.127.0, current 0.127.0`. So Windows needs no handoff release.
  - **Running the dry run safely**: a test app never starts on the person's LOCALAPPDATA or APPDATA (`assertTestEnv`): batch P's prototype run did, and left its 200 MB test setup in the owner's real `%LOCALAPPDATA%\ompdesk-updater\pending\` (found and removed 13 Nov 2026; his v0.127.0 `installer.exe` kept). The one-click setup restarts the app through Explorer, which carries none of the setup's environment, so the test setup starts it through a launcher that sets the test profile and refuses without one.
- **SX. The shell port** (batch SX, 13 Nov 2026, the owner: move the Electron-bound TypeScript of `docs/rust-remaining-units.md` §4 out of the Node child into the shell, in parallel). Five slices, one session each, each on `omp/agent/shell-port-<slice>` stacked on `omp/agent/shell-port-base` (which sits on S's branch); they land with S, in this order, each after its own batch run. The contract is `crates/shell/PROTOCOL.md` §15: a module per subsystem in `crates/shell/src/modules/` (the `Module` trait, the `MODULES` list, `Ctx` scoped to the module's namespace, the core reached through Node's `core.call` relay), its pure rules in the library beside `captured.json` from the TypeScript, a typed client per namespace in `src/main/shell-host/modules/<namespace>.ts`, the TypeScript deleted in the same commits. Local checks: `cargo check` of the shell crate only; everything else on GitHub's runners.
  - **SX1 chrome** (`chrome.*`; was `window.ts`, `menu.ts`, `tray.ts`, `app-quit.ts`, `deep-link.ts`, `gpu-fallback.ts`, `hang-watchdog.ts`, `app-icon.ts` and their rule files `window-bounds`, `window-show`, `renderer-recovery`, `editable-context-menu`, `quit-watchdog`, `gpu-fallback-core`, `launch-guard(-core)`, `hang-watchdog-core`/`-worker`, the menu functions of `tray-labels.ts`, `parseLaunchArgv`, the quit rules of `quit-guard.ts`; branch `omp/agent/shell-port-chrome`): moved. The shell's `chrome` module (`crates/shell/src/modules/chrome/{window,menu,tray,quit,deep_link,gpu,hang}.rs`, pure rules in `src/chrome_core/`) makes every app window (ids from 2^32, adopted by the shim with `win.adopted`), builds the application menu, the text field's context menu and the tray, approves and confirms quits with the 30 s exit watchdog, takes deep links, open-file, second launches and the Dock click, decides the GPU fallback and the crash-loop guard before CEF starts, and watches Node's main thread from a thread of its own. The wire is PROTOCOL.md §15.5; Node's side is `src/main/shell-host/modules/chrome-{window,menu,tray,quit,deep-link,gpu,hang}.ts` (joined by `chrome.ts`). The shim lost `Menu`, `MenuItem`, `Tray`, the `menu.*`/`tray.*` frames, `app.setAsDefaultProtocolClient`, the `activate`, `second-instance`, `open-url`, `open-file` events, `app.quitRequested` and `app.init`'s `disableHardwareAcceleration` (rule 6). Proof: the TypeScript was run on captured inputs before it was deleted (`chrome_core/captured/<area>.json`: window 197, menu 287, tray 321, quit 441, deeplink 339, gpu 814, hang 40 steps) and the Rust tests replay them; the parity suites `shell-host/modules/chrome-{window,menu,tray,quit,deep-link,gpu,hang}.test.ts` run against the real shell. Differences (PROTOCOL.md §15.5 Deviations): the renderer entry's identity on Windows is size, mtime and creation time; `chrome.open` makes the window asynchronously (`createWindow`/`spawnWindow` are async); deep links restore a minimized window and are queued (≤64) until `chrome.linksReady`; the quit watchdog asks Node's forced-exit hooks (2 s) then exits 0; the tray's labels follow the label table's language; the context menu is shown without a Node round trip and "Add to dictionary" works; the a helper's end comes from the shell's one child watcher (`childwatch.rs`): Windows reads the exit code, macOS the wait status through `kqueue` `NOTE_EXITSTATUS` without reaping (`app.childProcessGone` on macOS: exit 0 `clean-exit`, another code `abnormal-exit` with that code, SIGINT/SIGKILL/SIGTERM `killed`, any other signal `crashed` with the signal's number), so a GPU process that exits cleanly never counts as a crash, and a helper whose end could not be watched is not reported; GPU adapter identity comes from the registry on Windows only, so on macOS only the 7-day cool-off brings hardware back; the hang watchdog is a shell thread that keeps running while Node is blocked; under `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS=1` the shell posts no notifications. Stays TypeScript: Node's teardown (`finalize`, the `settled` wait, `before-quit`/`will-quit` handlers), because it closes Node's own tabs and sidecars; the tab inventory `assessQuitRisk` in `quit-guard.ts`; `TRAY_LABELS` and `aggregateTrayStatus`/`aggregateProgress` in `tray-labels.ts` (SX5 moves the tables); `isExistingDirectory` in `launch-argv.ts`; `nativeThemeSource`, `sanitizeChromeColors` and `TOP_BAR_HEIGHT` in `window-chrome.ts`; `WindowManager`'s records (tabs per window). CI: `shell-check`, `shell-parity` on Windows and macOS, and the e2e specs `desktop.e2e.ts` (menu through `chrome.menuProbe`), `desktop-tabs.e2e.ts` (the Dock click through `chrome.activate`), `desktop-surfaces.e2e.ts` (tray, Mica), `first-launch-window.e2e.ts`, `window-theme.e2e.ts` and `interrupted-resume.e2e.ts` (the quit).
  - **SX2 preview** (`preview.*`; `preview/**` of §4 and `web/preview-{stream,input,dialog,pacer}.ts`): not started.
  - **SX3 services** (`services.*`; the Electron services of `ipc.ts` and `ssh/ipc.ts`, their IPC handlers staying in Node's `IpcRegistry`): not started.
  - **SX4 updater** (`updater.*`; `updater.ts`, `update-auth.ts`, `nsis-updater.ts`, `updater-state.ts`): not started.
  - **SX5 boot** (`boot.*`; `index.ts`'s Electron wiring, `power/**`, `i18n.ts`, `i18n-text.ts` and `tray-labels.ts`'s tables, `sidecar-factory.ts`'s paths): not started.
  - Stay in Node for the batch (PROTOCOL.md §15.4): `gui-runtime.jsonl`'s writer (`runtime-log.ts`, reached from Rust by `log.runtime`) and page IPC's dispatch (`web/ipc-registry.ts`, `IPC_POLICY`). The label table reaches the shell from Node (`i18n.labels`) only until SX5 moves i18n into the shell; modules read it through `shell_core::labels` before and after. Parity suites run against the real shell (`shell-host/modules/test-shell.ts`): Windows in `ci.yml`'s `shell-parity` (batch), macOS in `mac-checks.yml`'s `mac-smoke` (the landing run's `mac=true`).
  - When all five landed, Node runs only the §2.7 repair path and the typed clients; deleting Node and the shim is S's last step (the S entry above).
